# Seed 42 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - not small: the knee moved (Iteration 209)

`seed 42 tag2 6h then experiment compare-fronts.py --rounds 6 --frontier
--cpus 2,8`, commit 13259cce (the after-seed-41 batch: the forms and run-time
immediates, rtloop=2, opcall in every band, regpin, the attic and the knee
kept, crossover 0.25 with linked genes, speed ties within 2%), started 21:15;
the run and the comparison (session 37) packed by 02:21. 15,352 designs,
15,241 alive.

Session 37's own verdict - the first run judged by the five measures as it
ran (results/gp-deep-dive.md): fastest -7.0%, smallest +0.18%, memory +6 KB,
hypervolume +4.8%, best Q +0.5% - not small. The Q-best design of all runs is
seed 42's 9a3de58ebe: 0.211 at 26,472 bytes, 180 KB, Q 0.223 of s6's. By the
extremes alone (the rule before Iteration 189) the run would have been small.

Deaths: 111 (seed 41: 907) - 76 reach limit, 35 short branch out of range
(fixed in the after-seed-42 batch), no corpus death (opcall's fix holds). The
final front: 155 designs; of the 133 made in this run, 98 by mutation alone, 28
by borrowing, 7 by crossover; 8 via the attic; rtloop 0/1/2: 33/66/56;
regpin 19, all at the fast end (0.179-0.187).

The report as the run wrote it follows.

# Evolved VM designs

15352 designs evaluated, 15241 alive. Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): bff70e0010, speed 0.194, binary + image 26,544, memory 180 KB - Q 0.206 of s6-cv8b's.
